# Rule 8 (NSA/CSA): plan-time host ints, SWA caller-owned workspace and THD ragged offsets; wrappers unchanged

[Upstream PR](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183) · state: open · merged: False

## Original PR description

## Before submitting

- [x] I agree to license this contribution under the terms of [LICENSE.txt](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/develop/LICENSE.txt).
- [x] I ran `pre-commit run` and committed any formatting changes.
- [x] I reviewed the Hard Rules in the `AGENTS.md` for each directory this PR touches (see [root AGENTS.md § Reviewing a PR](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/develop/AGENTS.md#reviewing-a-pr-human-or-agent)) and my changes comply, or I explain the exception below.
- [x] I added GitHub labels: one `cat-*`, one or more `area:*` / `op:*`, and one `orig-*` (see [label list](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/labels)).

## Affected area

FE OSS kernels or CuTeDSL: `python/cudnn/native_sparse_attention/**` (TopKReduction, SlidingWindowAttention, CompressionAttention), `python/cudnn/csa/**`, `test/python/fe_api/{nsa,csa}/**`, `docs/fe-oss-apis/nsa.md`.

## Summary

**Stacked on #1181** (the Rule 8 core PR: `WorkspaceCarver` in `api_base`, the R9 detector). **For the NSA / CSA owners to review**: plan-level classes only; every `*_wrapper` keeps its signature and behaviour.

Rule 8 (#1167) for NSA / CSA: **no host blocking on `compile()` / `execute()`, and a plan allocates nothing on the caller's behalf.**

- **Host ints at plan time (R10).** TopKReduction / SWA / CSA sizes that were read from device tensors at execute (`.item()` / implicit syncs) become plan-time host integers (`max_seqlen_*` envelopes) validated in `check_support()`.
- **SWA (R2).** `SlidingWindowAttention.execute(..., workspace=)`: the per-call scratch is carved from the caller's workspace (`scratch_workspace_bytes()`); THD ragged offsets are caller-owned and built once (`NSA.packed_thd_ragged_offsets(seq_len_q, seq_len_kv, ...)`) instead of five per-execute `cumsum`/`cat` tensors; `_check_ragged_offset` requires contiguous offsets (the graph declares unit strides). The `sliding_window_attention_wrapper` allocates the workspace and derives the offsets per call under the launch stream and guards the THD-without-seq-lens case with a clear `ValueError`. `docs/fe-oss-apis/nsa.md` builds the offsets before the constructor in the class-API example.
- **Detectors (R9).** `test_nsa_swa_execute_on_a_side_stream_allocates_nothing` (caller allocates on a side stream; execute allocates nothing), allocation-count and sync detectors on the touched classes; `test_NSA_compression_attention.py` restores the sync-debug mode it sets.

## Why

Rule 8's blocking clause: an `APIBase.execute` must be capturable and must not serialize the host on the device (`.item()` and implicit syncs did both). #1168 already removed the `torch.cuda.synchronize()` from SWA and put its per-call scratch on the handle's stream; this PR finishes the module.

## Related issues

#1167, #1168 (merged), #1181; supersedes the NSA / CSA parts of #1171.

## API and compatibility impact

**Wrappers: signatures and behaviour unchanged.** A wrapper-compatibility audit against develop found and fixed, in aac31e014: `sliding_window_attention_wrapper` requiring `max_seq_len_q/kv` for THD (derived from the seq-len tensors again when not passed; the kwargs stay as overrides), allocating offsets and workspace on torch's current stream instead of the launch stream, and rejecting non-contiguous caller offsets (made contiguous in the wrapper); `topk_reduction_wrapper` rejecting a row-major `(T, H_q)` LSE (staged into the kernel layout before the memo key) and requiring `max_s_q/max_s_k` for THD (inferred from `cum_seqlen` as before). Wrapper-level regression tests added beside the kept class-level declines. `api_index.txt`: no change from this PR. Downstream survey (TransformerEngine, Megatron-LM, flashinfer, vLLM, SGLang): the only NSA/CSA consumer found is Megatron-LM via `cudnn.csa.compressor.*_wrapper`.

Plan-level classes: `SlidingWindowAttention.execute` gains `workspace=` (required when `scratch_workspace_bytes() > 0`) and takes caller-built THD ragged offsets (contiguous); THD plans need the seq-len envelopes at construction; `NSA.packed_thd_ragged_offsets` is new.

## Testing

At a101e83bb (on top of the core branch; venv cuDNN 9.27, DSL 4.8 site, `-n 4`, `-m "L0 or L1"`): `fe_api/nsa fe_api/csa` **B200 173 passed, 3 skipped**; **H100 6 passed, 168 skipped** (SM100-only kernels). `api_index` scanner (every module but `cudnn.jax`) matches the package. Per-slice results before integration are in the superseded #1171 description (nsa + dsa 389 passed on B200).

<sub>note to self: claude::61d24ed2-7c90-4a97-9cbb-b91ae18136fd — "cuDNN backend exclusion on Blackwell" · cwd /home/scratch.yanxu_libs/flashinfer · workspace /data/yanxu/worktrees/scratch-gpu/fe-wt-r8nsa</sub>

## Summary by CodeRabbit

* **New Features**
  * Added caller-managed workspace sizing and execution support across grouped GEMM, attention, SDPA, MoE, and linear-attention APIs. Convenience wrappers allocate workspace automatically.
  * Added packed THD ragged-offset construction and expert output-pointer utilities.
  * Added optional device-side validation for offsets and pointer values.
  * Added support for prebuilt SM80 RoPE tables and stream-aware tensor staging.

* **Documentation**
  * Clarified workspace alignment, lifetime, concurrency, CUDA graph capture, and validation requirements.
  * Documented sparse-attention sequence-length envelopes and layout constraints.

* **Bug Fixes**
  * Missing or invalid workspace is rejected before kernel launches or output writes.
  * Improved handling of non-contiguous linear-attention inputs and staged layouts.
  * Top-K reduction enforces supported LSE layouts and THD sequence-length envelopes.

- YangXu1990uiuc · COMMENTED · 2026-09-22T07:17:54Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#pullrequestreview-5275114082)

**Codex bot review · model: `gpt-6-astra`**

Reviewed `1826ee51fc11` against base `9e9050c4013e`.

One high-risk P1 remains in the NSA slice: moving THD offset generation into the wrapper lost the effective cuDNN-handle stream. Please address it before merging. Two nonblocking compatibility items are also below. All 87 changed files are byte-identical to their versions in the reviewed #1174 stack, so the split itself does not invalidate that evidence or resolve its findings. In particular, the inherited core's eager-JAX scratch-lifetime issue remains applicable to #1181 (https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1174#pullrequestreview-5274694451); the shared-carver item is recorded at https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1172#pullrequestreview-5274566515.

**[P1 · blocks approval] Generate THD offsets on the effective handle stream** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/1826ee51fc11915c57f8e07fa37cece8effa2b9f/python/cudnn/native_sparse_attention/sliding_window_attention/api.py#L613))

A supported call with cudnn_handle bound to a side stream and stream=None generates these offsets on the ambient Torch stream, but SlidingWindowAttention.execute launches on the handle's stream. There is no dependency between them. The old code used stream_context(cudnn.get_stream(cudnn_handle), device) while deriving offsets. This is high-risk P1 because stale ragged offsets become addresses into Q/K/V/O, allowing incorrect reads or writes. Resolve the effective handle/launch stream before producing offsets and allocating temporary workspace, then use it consistently for cold and cached calls. Keep the explicit-stream override behavior. The side-stream class test does not cover this allocation-owning wrapper path; add a handle-only wrapper regression with a delayed producer and an explicit-stream control. This directly exercises Python Hard Rule 5.

Evidence: Reproduced on exact head 1826ee51f, FP16 packed THD, B=2, S=128, H=2, D=64, left_bound=64, SM100. After a successful real warm execution, the probe delays the real offset producer and replaces only the final pygraph.execute consumer with a bounded copy of the three Q offsets. The real class execute still resolves the handle and validates arguments. Handle-only reads [-1234567,-1234567,-1234567] while production is pending; final offsets are [0,16384,32768]. Explicitly passing the handle's stream reads the correct values in order. The same probe on develop 9e9050c passes both arms. No attention kernel was run using poisoned offsets. Attribution: Introduced by the NSA ownership refactor, originally present in #1171 and carried unchanged into this replacement. The develop control establishes that the effective-handle stream behavior regressed.

**[P2] Append workspace after the existing execute parameters** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/1826ee51fc11915c57f8e07fa37cece8effa2b9f/python/cudnn/native_sparse_attention/sliding_window_attention/api.py#L427))

workspace is inserted before current_stream, cudnn_handle and skip_compile, shifting the existing positional API. Move it after skip_compile so callers keep their old bindings; keyword callers remain unaffected. This is the root guide's append-only signature convention and the same unresolved item previously noted on the superseded stack, not a new discovery from splitting it.

Evidence: AST comparison with develop: the old suffix is stats_ragged_offset_tensor, current_stream, cudnn_handle, skip_compile; this head inserts workspace before current_stream. The NSA file is identical to the earlier reviewed version. Attribution: Introduced in #1171; previously reported on #1172 and retained in this replacement.

**[P2] Preserve the existing THD wrapper call without max-length hints** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/1826ee51fc11915c57f8e07fa37cece8effa2b9f/python/cudnn/native_sparse_attention/sliding_window_attention/api.py#L554))

The PR says wrappers retain their behavior, but an existing sliding_window_attention_wrapper(q,k,v,seq_len_q_tensor=...,seq_len_kv_tensor=...) call now raises unless the caller supplies two new max_seq_len hints. Those hints did not exist on the develop wrapper, which inferred the envelope. Keep the class's explicit plan-time contract while retaining a documented compatibility fallback on the wrapper when hints are omitted; recipe R10 explicitly permits that wrapper-only fallback. Keep the resolved envelope in the memo key. The new test currently pins the breaking behavior rather than testing the promised wrapper compatibility.

Evidence: Develop's wrapper signature ends at stream and derives max sequence lengths during construction; this head appends optional hints but immediately rejects the old THD invocation. test_nsa_swa_wrapper_thd_requires_max_seq_len confirms that rejection and passed in the exact-head NSA suite. Attribution: Introduced in the NSA slice and carried into this replacement; inconsistent with this PR's stated wrapper-compatibility scope.

Validation: Exact-head NSA suite with explicit -m 'L0 or L1': 26 passed, 2 skipped on SM100, Torch 2.13, DSL 4.7, cuDNN 9.26. Confirmed the imported package belongs to this checkout and native sources match the built binding donor. The bounded stream probe above covers the wrapper/handle combination absent from that suite. SWA performance spot check against develop 9e9050c, FP16 (B,H,S,D)=(2,2,128,64) and (2,8,512,64), left_bound=64, same SM100 GPU/environment. Warm class-API CPU enqueue medians were about 155/177 us before versus 129/130 us after; captured GPU time about 22.04/58.05 us before versus 22.04/58.14 us after. Seven timing batches per version; caller workspace is allocated outside the head timer. O and LSE match an independent reference, and captured replay passes after changing V and poisoning O/LSE/workspace. These two cases show no material regression, not a general performance guarantee. Owner-triggered exact-head pipeline 69196717 is already running. Builds/API-index checks passed; selected GPU lanes were pending/running at inspection, and OSS lanes were skipped. No duplicate CI requested; this high-risk finding should be fixed before spending additional CI capacity.

Limitations: The stream reproducer demonstrates stale metadata at the real graph boundary using a bounded consumer; it deliberately does not execute attention through invalid offsets. It does not claim an observed illegal-memory-access crash. A separate scratch-reuse experiment did not reproduce allocator reuse and is not used as evidence of scratch corruption. Independent current-head tests and performance here cover NSA on SM100. Inherited-core evidence is reused only after byte-for-byte comparison with the reviewed stack; the core's unresolved findings remain. Full CI is incomplete.

Comment only: GitHub does not allow reviewing one's own PR with approval/request-changes.

I am withholding approval for the high-risk P1 findings above and leaving a COMMENT. Please address them before merging.

Please address this before merging. The inherited core JAX scratch-lifetime blocker also remains unchanged. Two previously reported API-compatibility items remain nonblocking.

- **[P1 · blocks approval] Still open:** [Generate THD offsets on the effective handle stream](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#pullrequestreview-5275114082).

- **[P2] Still open:** [Append workspace after existing execute parameters](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#pullrequestreview-5275114082).

Covers SWA/CSA numerical reference checks, workspace/ragged-offset contracts and TopK layout rejection. TopK smoke tests explicitly lack a numerical reference and are not counted as such. Independent safe stream-selection probe: cold and cached handle-only THD calls generate offsets on stream 0 and consume them on the supplied side stream; the explicit-stream control generates and consumes on the same side stream. The probe synchronized after generation to prevent invalid memory access, so it establishes stream selection rather than a fresh corruption reproduction. The earlier review's delayed-consumer reproduction remains separately attributed. Reviewed all nine changed files, affected wrapper/class callers, CSA compile-envelope use and TopK's fixed LSE strides. Core affected JAX paths are unchanged from the independently reviewed #1181. No additional CI requested while blockers remain.

Limitations: No fresh parent/head performance comparison or Hopper/Rubin run. Earlier performance measurements are not represented as this review's own results. Focused passing tests do not resolve the stream-ordering or inherited core lifetime blockers.

I am withholding approval for the high-risk P1 findings above and leaving a COMMENT. Please address them before merging.

> **Validate the THD workspace before taking its pointer.**

> `_scratch_base` checks only `numel() * element_size()` and pointer alignment. `bind_thd` checks only the workspace pointer alignment before placing it in the frame. >          base = workspace.data_ptr()
> ```

**Codex bot review · model: `gpt-6-astra`**

Reviewed `81d395f023bd` against base `93388ee5e197`.

The previous NSA-specific findings are resolved: handle-only SWA now orders offset generation correctly, the old execute positional prefix is preserved, and omitted THD length hints work again. The new Top-K LSE staging path has a separate, reproduced lifetime regression on explicit streams, however. Please address that high-risk P1 and the remaining inherited workspace-cap finding before merging.

**[P1 · blocks approval] Keep the original LSE alive until its staging copy finishes** ([source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/blob/81d395f023bde0272a7c8a2a53c5b053dacf2aae/python/cudnn/native_sparse_attention/top_k/api.py#L35))

When a caller supplies row-major THD LSE and an explicit side stream, this new contiguous copy reads the original allocation asynchronously. The wrapper replaces lse_tensor with the staged result, but the original allocation is never recorded on that stream. Releasing the caller's original LSE after the wrapper returns can therefore recycle its storage before the queued copy reads it. In a warmed public-wrapper reproduction, overwriting that reused allocation changed both Top-K scores and indices; recording the original LSE on the launch stream prevented reuse and restored exact agreement. Please record the source allocation's stream usage before dropping it, or retain it until the copy completes. Add a delayed-copy/reuse test that releases the original row-major LSE; keeping inputs alive through synchronization hides this case. This is a high-risk P1 because a supported wrapper call can silently select different blocks.

Evidence: Exact-head SM100 reproduction: FP16 Q/K, FP32 LSE shaped (8192,4,1), two THD batches with S_q=4096 and S_k=128, H_q/H_k=4/1, D=128, k=16. Both wrapper and overwrite kernel were warmed. With the side stream still pending, the original LSE address was reused and overwritten; finite-score max error was 198665.90625 and indices differed. The source-record_stream control had no premature reuse and scores/indices matched exactly. The same probe against source-verified develop 9e9050c passed both arms, including actual address reuse while the consumer remained pending. This specifically attributes the regression to moving the staging read onto the side stream without protecting its source. Attribution: Introduced by the new LSE staging compatibility fix in aac31e014; independently compared with develop. Separate from the now-fixed SWA offset-ordering issue.

- **[P1 · blocks approval] Still open:** [Inherited parent issue: retain pending workspaces beyond the capacity boundary](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#pullrequestreview-5275114082).

Validation: Exact-head NSA L0/L1 suite: 28 passed, 2 expected skips for unsupported BSHD selection attention. Includes restored omitted-envelope and row-major-LSE wrapper behavior, strided ragged offsets, class rejection contracts and side-stream no-allocation checks. Torch 2.13, DSL 4.7, cuDNN 9.26 on one SM100 GPU; source import and native-binding compatibility verified. Re-ran the original bounded SWA offset-ordering probe at the real graph boundary. Both handle-only and explicit-stream calls now consume [0,16384,32768] in order; the previous poisoned-offset failure is resolved. AST comparison confirms workspace is appended after the full pre-existing execute signature. The independent Top-K delayed-consumer experiment above exercises the unmodified public wrapper and real kernel, with a source-record_stream control and a develop comparison. Baseline source files were checked against 9e9050c, and both source imports were verified. This validation checks lifetime/correctness, not GPU performance. The parent lifetime issue is improved but not resolved: the shared helper still drops live entries at its 64-buffer cap. On identical source, a real-CUDA-event/weak-reference probe released two of 66 retained non-Torch stand-ins while the first completion fence was NOT_READY; the larger-cap diagnostic control retained all 66. This is premature-reference-release evidence, not a new end-to-end JAX corruption run. The latest owner CI request failed before pipeline launch. No additional broad CI requested while the demonstrated lifetime blockers remain. The older pipeline is not evidence for this new head; the previous SWA class-level performance comparison is retained as prior evidence only, with no fresh wrapper speed claim.

Limitations: Independent validation here covers NSA on SM100, not a full CSA/multi-architecture sweep. The Top-K regression checks a controlled explicit-stream reuse sequence; it is not an end-to-end model benchmark.

Comment only: GitHub does not allow reviewing one's own PR with approval/request-changes.

I am withholding approval for the high-risk P1 findings above and leaving a COMMENT. Please address them before merging.

## Walkthrough

The change moves scratch and staging storage to caller-owned workspace across grouped GEMM, MoE, attention, NSA, and SDPA paths. APIs expose workspace sizing and validate supplied buffers. Wrappers, benchmarks, documentation, and tests are updated for these execution contracts.

**Workspace execution contracts**

|Layer / File(s)|Summary|
|---|---|
|**Grouped GEMM APIs and shared workspace handling** <br> `python/cudnn/api_base.py`, `python/cudnn/_torch_stream.py`, `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/*`, `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/discrete_grouped/*`, `python/cudnn/tensor_adapter.py`|Grouped GEMM APIs report scratch sizes and pass caller-owned workspace to compiled kernels. Shared helpers validate alignment, retain buffers through asynchronous execution, and provide stream-aware copies.|
|**WGRAD and MoE execution** <br> `python/cudnn/gemm/cutedsl/grouped/wgrad/*`, `python/cudnn/gemm/frost/*`, `benchmark/*`|WGRAD adds workspace handling and expert-pointer table support. MoE validates workspace before output initialization. Benchmarks allocate workspace before timed runs.|
|**Attention, NSA, and SDPA paths** <br> `python/cudnn/linear_attention/*`, `python/cudnn/native_sparse_attention/*`, `python/cudnn/sdpa/*`|These paths use workspace-backed staging and scratch. NSA adds explicit THD ragged offsets and plan-time sequence envelopes. Top-K checks LSE layouts, and SM80 SDPA accepts a prebuilt RoPE table.|
|**Documentation and validation coverage** <br> `docs/fe-oss-apis/*`, `python/cudnn/AGENTS.md`, `test/python/*`|Documentation describes updated workspace, pointer, and validation contracts. Tests cover workspace requirements, capture, allocations, synchronization, staging, and related API behavior.|

<!-- change_assessment_start -->
**Priority:** ⬇️ Low































**Estimated code review effort:** 5 (Critical) | ~90 minutes

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-09-22T21:53:16Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#issuecomment-5784794377)

Re the Codex review on 1826ee51f (all three items predate the current head): P1 (THD offsets on the effective handle stream) — fixed in aac31e014 / 8d5a09879: the launch stream (`stream`, else the handle's) is resolved first and the offsets, the workspace and both envelope reductions run under it; regression added in 851f48ce4: `test_nsa_swa_wrapper_handle_only_thd_derives_offsets_on_the_handle_stream` (handle re-streamed to a side stream, `stream=None`, ambient torch stream held busy by a long kernel; explicit-stream control). P2 (workspace before current_stream) — fixed in ebc0950e6: `workspace` is appended after `skip_compile`. P2 (THD wrapper without max-length hints) — fixed in aac31e014: the wrapper derives `max_seq_len_*` from `seq_len_*` when omitted (documented .item() fallback, R10) and keys its memo on the result; `test_nsa_swa_wrapper_thd_derives_max_seq_len_and_restrides_offsets`. Also in 851f48ce4: the SWA ragged-offset and top-k LSE staging copies record the original on the launch stream first (R1 staging, see #1181 b1947089b).

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-09-26T11:52:58Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#issuecomment-5846041446)

CI follow-up for reviewed head `a874740f2eb6`: pipeline **69914086** has finished with **32 successful jobs and one failed job** (plus 18 skipped / 5 manual).

The SM100 SDPA failure needs follow-up; its timeout is not evidence of a runner-only failure. Job **456341775** first hit the 25-minute per-test watchdog in `test_thd_output_row_stride_above_int32_reaches_device_descriptors`, with the main thread inside `PreparedTHD.execute -> self.spec.native.execute` (`prepared.py:771`). That worker was replaced; the job later reached its 80-minute limit without a final pytest summary. The traceback does not identify which D parameter was active or establish why the native call stalled.

I freshly reran that exact test at this head for D128/D256/D512: **3 passed**, including numerical output and CUDA Graph replay with the physical row stride above 2^32. This was a single-process local SM100 run with Torch 2.13 / cuDNN 9.26 / DSL 4.7; CI used four workers and Torch 2.14 / cuDNN 9.28 / DSL 4.7. The local pass therefore does not clear the CI hang. The test and `prepared.py` execution path involved are shared with current develop, rather than NSA-specific additions. Please retain this as unresolved validation and check the same case under the CI stack/concurrency before treating the lane as green.

Verified completed lane logs: SM103 SDPA **6,717 passed / 1,934 skipped**; SM107 SDPA **3,250 passed / 2,077 skipped**; SM120 SDPA **4,052 passed / 4,599 skipped**; SM100 linear **1,705 passed / 170 skipped / 8 xfailed**. The remaining executed jobs also succeeded. No additional pipeline was started.

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-09-30T23:09:27Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#issuecomment-5921282923)

Pipeline `70766194` has finished for `58055b1780ba7b8c50d6046d529ac825f8b10165`: **38 jobs passed, 9 failed, 13 skipped and 4 manual**. There are no remaining running or pending jobs. This is not a full-green result.

- The SM90 CuTeDSL lane has the 11 DSA host-sync detector failures already detailed in [the review](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#pullrequestreview-5371985590). Preserve that detector and remove the runtime host reads.
- The SM107 CuTeDSL lane has the shared GLU test helper's missing workspace, already explained [on #1182](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1182#issuecomment-5920435576).
- SM80 SDPA has one failure: the autouse workspace shim supplies storage to `test_workspace_rejected_before_copy[missing]`, defeating its intended negative input. SM120 SDPA has two stale workspace expectations: the THD scratch count and the split-path error-message regex. These are the same test-integration issues [described on #1182](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1182#issuecomment-5921128197); keep the actual missing/undersized-workspace rejection coverage when updating them.
- SM100 SDPA hit the job time limit after worker losses near the end of collection; SM107 SDPA also timed out, with repeated stacks in backward graph plan construction. Both leave incomplete coverage. These logs alone do not establish the root cause or demonstrate a passing SDPA regression.
- The three dev-backend lanes report MoE backend `BAD_PARAM` failures, with current-stream GNN numerical failures also present on SM80/SM90. I have not established base-versus-head attribution for those failures.

No duplicate pipeline was started. Fix the already identified test integration issues and triage the incomplete SDPA lanes before treating CI as qualified; the scoped native validation from the review remains separate evidence.

- YangXu1990uiuc · comment · 2026-10-01T01:38:37Z · [source](https://github.com/NVIDIA/cudnn-frontend/pull/1183#issuecomment-5922982434)

One actionable CI follow-up from pipeline 70807171 on `2504d418`: the SM107 OSS job reports **1 failed, 4391 passed, 2260 skipped**. The failure is a test expectation in `test_split_k_workspace_must_be_a_cuda_buffer_on_the_launch_device`: the implementation correctly raises `ValueError("... workspace must be on cuda:0, got cuda:1")`, but the test requires `match="device"`.

Please align that test's regex with the actual wrong-device diagnostic (for example `workspace must be on cuda:`), retaining the check that a buffer on the wrong GPU is rejected. This is a nonblocking test integration issue, not evidence of a failed workspace guard or a numerical regression. That GAB test is present in the CI integration tree, not in this public PR head, so I am flagging it as a CI follow-up rather than attributing its introduction to this diff.

The remaining pipeline lanes are still running; this is not an all-green CI conclusion.

**Require 64-byte workspace alignment.** The 128-byte carve stride preserves the base alignment for every chunk, but cuTensorMap requires each descriptor address to be 64-byte aligned. A workspace view aligned only to 16 bytes passes the current check. The THD path can then pass a misaligned descriptor to TMA and violate the execution contract.

```
-        if flat.data_ptr() % 16 != 0:
-            raise ValueError(f"cudnn: {owner} workspace must be at least 16-byte aligned; got data_ptr=0x{flat.data_ptr():x}")
+        if flat.data_ptr() % 64 != 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"cudnn: {owner} workspace must be at least 64-byte aligned; got data_ptr=0x{flat.data_ptr():x}")
```

**Retain the JAX workspace after `execute()` returns.**

The BF16 JAX facade allocates the workspace, passes it to `_bf16_api.execute()`, and launches `_compiled_kernel` on `current_stream`. `execute()` creates only a local view, so JAX may reclaim or reuse the workspace before the asynchronous launch completes. This can corrupt results or cause a runtime failure.

```diff
         nbytes = self.scratch_workspace_bytes()
         ws_view = Workspace(workspace, nbytes, type(self).__name__).take(nbytes, "uint8")
+        if not is_torch_tensor(workspace):
+            # Immutable frameworks (JAX) have no record_stream: keep the caller's buffer
+            # referenced until the next execute so it outlives the asynchronous launch
+            # (same as _live_b_ptrs).
+            self._live_workspace = workspace
         self._compiled_kernel(
```

**Retain the JAX workspace in the eager facade path.**

The eager JAX facade passes `allocate_wrapper_workspace(...)` to `GroupedGemmGluBf16API.execute()`. That method creates a temporary view and passes only `ws_view.data_ptr()` to the asynchronous kernel. It retains `_live_b_ptrs` for immutable frameworks, but not `workspace`. JAX can recycle the workspace while the kernel still accesses it, causing invalid memory access or a crash.

This does not apply to the separate XLA custom-call entry point, which passes workspace as a JAX output buffer.

<details>
<summary>🐛 Proposed fix</summary>

```diff
         nbytes = self.scratch_workspace_bytes()
```

<**Retain the JAX workspace through the asynchronous launch.**

The JAX wrapper passes `allocate_wrapper_workspace(...)` to `GroupedGemmSm100.execute`. `Workspace` stores only the raw pointer and metadata. The BF16 implementation passes `ws_view.data_ptr()` to `_compiled_kernel` and does not retain the non-Torch workspace owner. The JAX workspace can therefore be released or reused after `execute` returns while the kernel still uses the pointer.

Add the same retention branch used by the comparable JAX paths:

```python
if not is_torch_tensor(workspace):
    self._live_workspace = workspace
```

**Do not discard workspaces with incomplete events.**

Line 211 retains entries whose events are not complete. Line 213 then keeps only the latest 63 event-bearing entries. With more than 64 overlapping launches, this releases an older `buffer` while its event still reports that the launch is in flight. A caching allocator can reuse that workspace and corrupt the active kernel.

Retain every incomplete entry. If retention must be bounded, release an entry only after its event completes or after a safe stream synchronization.

**Normalize non-contiguous `dO` before the packed backward graph.**

`dO` is normalized only when it has a zero stride. A transposed or padded `dO` with nonzero strides reaches `graph.execute` unchanged. The graph declares `dO` without strides, so the variant pack uses compact row-major offsets and can produce incorrect gradients.

<details>
<summary>🐛 Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-    if 0 in dO.stride():
+    if not dO.is_contiguous():
         dO = dO.contiguous()
```

</details>

<bold>Resolve the launch stream before inferring sequence envelopes.</bold>

The `.max().item()` reads occur before `launch` is resolved and before `stream_context` makes the caller-supplied stream current. If the sequence-length tensors were produced asynchronously on `stream`, the reductions can read stale data. These values are part of `cache_key` and are passed to `SlidingWindowAttention`, so the wrapper can cache or compile an envelope from stale lengths.

Resolve the effective launch stream first. Run both reductions under that stream. Use the same stream for the subsequent launch.

**Fence the final original buffer before synchronization.**

`torch.cuda.synchronize()` does not wait for the final buffer's event because `retain_workspace()` records that event only during the later `late` call. Its immediate query can therefore leave the final original buffer in `_live_workspaces`, causing the exact-list assertion to fail. Add a sentinel call before synchronization, then assert that the original buffers are absent and `late` is retained.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
+    sentinel = _EagerBuffer(_Desc.device)
+    retain_workspace(api, sentinel, stream)
     torch.cuda.synchronize()
     late = _EagerBuffer(_Desc.device)
     retain_workspace(api, late, stream)  # every earlier launch has completed: their buffers are released
-    assert [e.buffer for e in api._live_workspaces] == [late]
+    live = [e.buffer for e in api._live_workspaces]
+    assert not any(buffer is original for buffer in live for original in buffers)
+    assert any(buffer is late for buffer in live)
```

</details>

```suggestion
    torch.cuda.synchronize()
    late = _EagerBuffer(_Desc.device)

    live = [e.buffer for e in api._live_workspaces]
    assert not any(buffer is original for buffer in live for original in buffers)
    assert any(buffer is late for buffer in live)
```

Done in edc5c3c5c (#1181, merged forward): the pending cap is gone. An entry is released only when its own fence has completed; an entry whose fence could not be recorded is released once a later entry on the same stream has completed (stream order). **Bind the compacted `cu_seqlens` in the variant pack.**

Line 403 and Line 915 compact `cu_seqlens`, but the variant packs do not use the compacted tensor. They bind `cu`, and `cu` was captured before the compaction (Line 354 in forward, Line 874 in backward). So the variant packs at Line 486 and Line 999 still receive the original tensor.

```diff
+    cu = cu_seqlens
```

Apply the same line after Line 915 in `kda_bwd`.

**Record CUDA tensors even when the launch stream is current.**

`record_streams()` skips `tensor.record_stream()` whenever the provided stream is the current raw stream (lines 110–112). This prevents the caching allocator from learning that a tensor is needed on that stream. When `contiguous_on_stream()` queues a copy on that stream, the allocator can reuse the tensor's block on its original stream before the copy runs. If the caller releases the tensor, its memory is overwritten before the copy reads it.

Remove the current-stream early return. Record every CUDA source tensor when `stream` is not `None`.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
-    if handle not in DEFAULT_STREAM_HANDLES and handle == _raw_current_stream(torch, device):
-        return
     consumer = None
```

**Record `rope_freqs` on the launch stream before queuing RoPE table work.**

`_sm80_rope_table()` queues PyTorch operations (`.to()`, `.reshape()`, `.cos()`, `.sin()`, `.stack()`, `.contiguous()`) that read `rope_freqs` on the launch stream. The function returns without synchronizing. A caller can release `rope_freqs` after the wrapper returns, and the caching allocator can reuse its block before the queued work completes.

Before calling `_sm80_rope_table()`, record a same-device CUDA `rope_freqs` tensor on the effective launch stream using `record_streams()` from `cudnn._torch_stream`. This orders the allocator to retain the block until the queued work finishes.

<details>
<summary>Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
+from cudnn._torch_stream import stream_context, record_streams
 
     with _torch_stream_context(current_stream, q_tensor.device):
         workspace = torch.empty(ws_bytes, dtype=torch.uint8, device=q_tensor.device) if ws_bytes else None
+        if rope_freqs is not None and rope_freqs.is_cuda and rope_freqs.device == q_tensor.device:
+            record_streams(
+                (rope_freqs,),
+                current_stream,
+                q_tensor.device,
+            )
         rope_cs = _sm80_rope_table(rope_freqs, q_tensor.shape[-1] // 2, q_tensor.device, table_d2=api.flavor_d_qk // 2) if rope_freqs is not None else None
```

</details>

**Remove `RuntimeError` from the exception handler; it signals environment failures, not unsupported configurations.**

`check_support()` raises `RuntimeError` exclusively for environment and hardware limitations: when CUDA is unavailable (_bf16_api.py:264, _blockscaled_api.py:296) or when compute capability is below SM100 (_bf16_api.py:268, _blockscaled_api.py:301). It raises `ValueError` for configuration mismatches (e.g., _bf16_api.py:262). On a device with CUDA and SM100+ support, `RuntimeError` should never occur. If it does, it indicates an unexpected failure—such as a bug in device detection, broken CUDA setup, or runtime degradation—and should not be silently skipped. Catching `RuntimeError` alongside `ValueError` masks these unexpected failures. The test should only skip on expected unsupported configurations (`ValueError`), not on environment failures.

<details>
<summary>🐛 Suggested fix</summary>

```diff
    op = cudnn.GroupedGemmWgradSm100(**kw)
    try:
        supported = op.check_support()
-   except (ValueError, NotImplementedError, RuntimeError) as e:
+   except (ValueError, NotImplementedError) as e:
        pytest.skip(f"Unsupported testcase: {e}")
    if not supported:
        pytest.skip("Unsupported testcase")
```

</details>
